Hi, Doctor. Thanks so much for seeing me. Um, well, I guess I'm here because... I've been having a little trouble at work. It's probably nothing major, I mean, I'm a construction manager, so it's a busy job and I'm sure everyone gets a bit distracted, right? 

But... well, I'll start a safety briefing for the guys, or I'll be sitting in on a plan review, and I'm really trying hard to pay attention. I really am. But within just a few minutes, my mind is already off on three other job problems. And... I end up missing things. Things I know I definitely should have caught. Well, um... I wouldn't say "unusually" restless. I mean, I'm on a construction site all day, so I'm always moving around, checking on different crews, walking the site. It's just part of the job, you know?

Um, well... I guess I was always just a really energetic kid. I think a lot of little boys are like that, though, right? Just eager to get outside and play. 

But... yeah, I suppose if I'm being completely honest and thinking back on it... my teachers in elementary school did have to talk to my parents a fair bit. They, um, they would tell them that I couldn't really stay in my seat. And that I talked out of turn. It wasn't that I was trying to be disrespectful or a bad kid, I promise! I just... I don't know, I guess I'd get ahead of myself and just blurt things out. 

They also told my parents that I lost my homework a lot, and that I'd kind of just rush through the directions on assignments before I actually understood what we were supposed to be doing. I always felt pretty bad about it when my teachers had to bring it up. Does that... does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you were looking for?

Well, um... I really try my best to be a good listener, especially with my crew. Communication is huge on a job site, so I know how important it is. But... I guess, if I'm being totally honest, I do sometimes... jump the gun a little bit? 

It's never because I want to be rude, I promise! It's just... sometimes someone will start asking me something, and I think I already know exactly where their question is going, you know? So I'll sort of cut them off, just trying to be helpful and get them the answer quickly so we can keep moving. But then... well, because I answer too fast, I'll sometimes realize a second later that I completely missed the actual important part they hadn't even said yet. 

I always feel terrible when it happens, and I usually have to ask them to repeat themselves. I really don't mean to interrupt them. Does that... does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad manager or anything.

Well, um... I guess it does bleed over a little bit. I really try my best to be a good husband, I promise I do. But if I'm being completely honest... my wife, she's an absolute saint, but I think she's getting pretty worn down lately. I'll tell her I'm going to handle something around the house, and I fully intend to do it right then, but I just... I don't know, I lose track of it. So she ends up having to remind me over and over about things I said I would handle. I feel terrible about it, because I really don't want her to feel like she has to manage me, you know? Is it... is it normal to struggle with that kind of stuff at home? 

And, um... since we're talking about how it affects things, I should probably admit that I might have downplayed things at work a little earlier. I didn't want you to think I was a bad manager! But... it actually has gotten bad enough there that my employer recently brought up a formal concern with me. 

It wasn't that I was slacking off, I swear! It's just... I missed some important follow-ups, and my site coordination ended up being pretty poorly organized for a couple of weeks. They had to sit me down to talk about it. I'm working really hard to fix it so I don't let my crew or my bosses down again. Does that... does that answer your question okay? Well, um... I really try to be a professional, you know? Especially since I'm managing the crew, I want to set a good example. But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, sitting still for a long time is... well, it's a little tough for me. 

Like, when we have those long site meetings with the contractors and the higher-ups? Everyone else seems to just sit there through the whole thing, but I... I find myself getting up a lot. I try to make it look productive, I promise! I'll stand up to go get some coffee for people, or I'll step outside just to "check on something" real quick, or I'll just make some little excuse to move around the room. 

I don't do it to be disruptive, I swear. It's just... I get this feeling like I really need to stretch my legs or I'm going to jump out of my skin. But I always try to be polite about it! Does that... is that okay? I hope you don't think I'm just being disrespectful during meetings.

I hope you don't think I'm just being disrespectful during meetings.\n\n[Last Question]\nAs a child or teen, did you often argue with adults or refuse to follow rules?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. problems stacked up, I don’t know what to hit first so I jump around and everything runs late.\n- key: S007\n  name: Losing_Items\n  description: Often loses things necessary for tasks or activities.\n  manifestation: I’m always patting my pockets or digging through the truck for my keys, tape, notebook, marked-up drawings, or even the phone I just had in my hand a minute ago.\n- key: S011\n  name: Leaving_Seat\n  description: Often leaves seat in situations when remaining seated is expected.\n  manifestation: In meetings where everybody else can sit through the whole thing, I’m the guy standing up, walking to the coffee, stepping outside to check something, or making an excuse to move around.\n- key: S015\n  name: Excessive_Talking\n  description: Often talks excessively.\n  manifestation: Once I get going about a problem on the job, I keep talking and explaining from every angle, even when I can tell the other guys are trying to move on.\n- key: S016\n  name: Blurting_Answers\n  description: Often blurts out an answer before a question has been completed.\n  manifestation: I cut people off because I think I already know where the question is going, then I answer too fast and sometimes realize I missed the important part they hadn’t said yet.\n- key: S017\n  name: Waiting_Turn_Difficulty\n  description: Often has difficulty waiting his or her turn.\n  manifestation: Waiting for my turn on a call, at the permit office, or in a line drives me nuts, and I’m checking the time, shifting around, and fighting the urge to push ahead.\n- key: S018\n  name: someone working through paperwork and jump right in with my own point before I’ve been asked, and it rubs people the wrong way.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: Back in elementary school, teachers were already telling my parents I couldn’t stay in my seat, talked out of turn, lost homework, and rushed Oh, wow, no. No, definitely not. I... I would never do anything like that. Stealing or bullying? That's just... that's really not who I am, even back then.

Does that sound like what you're asking about?\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, were you often noticeably more hyperactive or impulsive than other kids?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I don't know, I guess I'd get ahead of myself and just blurt things out. \n\nThey also told my parents that I lost my homework a lot, and that I'd kind of just rush through the directions on assignments before I actually understood what we were supposed to be doing. I always felt pretty bad about it when my teachers had to bring it up. Does that... does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you still do things impulsively now, like interrupting people or blurting things out before thinking?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad manager or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do these attention and impulsivity problems also happen outside of work, such as at home or in social settings?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Well, um... yeah, I guess it did start pretty early. I really hope it doesn't sound like I was trying to be difficult, because I really wasn't! I always wanted my teachers to like me. Is that... is that something you see people for?\n\nDoctor: As an adult, do you often feel unusually restless or find it hard to sit still?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say \"unusually\" restless. I mean, I'm on a construction site all day, so I'm always moving around, checking on different crews, walking the site. It's just part of the job, you know? \n\nI guess... sometimes if I have to sit in the trailer for a long time doing paperwork or looking at blueprints, I might tap my foot a little, or feel like I need to get up and stretch my legs. But that's pretty normal for guys in my line of work who are used to being on their feet, right? I don't think it's anything too crazy. I mean, I can sit still if I really have to. Does that sound like what you're asking about?\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, were you often noticeably more hyperactive or impulsive than other kids?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I was always just a really energetic kid. I think a lot of little boys are like that, though, right? Just eager to get outside and play. \n\nBut... yeah, I suppose if I'm being completely honest and thinking back on it... my teachers in elementary school did have to talk to my parents a fair bit. They, um, they would tell them that I couldn't really stay in my seat. And that I talked out of turn. It wasn't that I was trying to be disrespectful or a bad kid, I promise! I just... I don't know, I guess I'd get ahead of myself and just blurt things out. \n\nThey also told my parents that I lost my homework a lot, and that I'd kind of just rush through the directions on assignments before I actually understood what we were supposed to be doing. I always felt pretty bad about it when my teachers had to bring it up. Does that... does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you still do things impulsively now, like interrupting people or blurting things out before thinking?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try my best to be a good listener, especially with my crew. Communication is huge on a job site, so I know how important it is. But... I guess, if I'm being totally honest, I do sometimes... jump the gun a little bit? \n\nIt's never because I want to be rude, I promise! It's just... sometimes someone will start asking me something, and I think I already know exactly where their question is going, you know? So I'll sort of cut them off, just trying to be helpful and get them the answer quickly so we can keep moving. But then... well, because I answer too fast, I'll sometimes realize a second later that I completely missed the actual important part they hadn't even said yet. \n\nI always feel terrible when it happens, and I usually have to ask them to repeat themselves. I really don't mean to interrupt them. Does that... does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad manager or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do these attention and impulsivity problems also happen outside of work, such as at home or in social settings?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it does bleed over a little bit. I really try my best to be a good husband, I promise I do. But if I'm being completely honest... my wife, she's an absolute saint, but I think she's getting pretty worn down lately. I'll tell her I'm going to handle something around the house, and I fully intend to do it right then, but I just... I don't know, I lose track of it. So she ends up having to remind me over and over about things I said I would handle. I feel terrible about it, because I really don't want her to feel like she has to manage me, you know? Is it... is it normal to struggle with that kind of stuff at home? \n\nAnd, um... since we're talking about how it affects things, I should probably admit that I might have downplayed things at work a little earlier. I didn't want you to think I was a bad manager! But... it actually has gotten bad enough there that my employer recently brought up a formal concern with me. \n\nIt wasn't that I was slacking off, I swear! It's just... I missed some important follow-ups, and my site coordination ended up being pretty poorly organized for a couple of weeks. They had to sit me down to talk about it. I'm working really hard to fix it so I don't let my crew or my bosses down again. Does that... does that answer your question okay? I hope you don't think less of me for letting things get like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often get into arguments with authority figures, like bosses, teachers, or police?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, not at all! I... I really try to be respectful of everyone, especially people in charge. I've always believed in, you know, treating people the way you want to be treated. \n\nI mean, sure, maybe I get a little frustrated sometimes if I don't understand why a certain decision was made at work, but I'd never just start an argument over it. I promise I'm not a difficult person to work with! I always try to keep my head down and do what's asked of me. I really respect my bosses and... well, anyone in authority, really. I hope that's the right answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm some kind of troublemaker.\n\nDoctor: In your day-to-day life now, do you often feel like you have to fidget, get up, or keep moving when you’re expected to stay seated?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to be a professional, you know? Especially since I'm managing the crew, I want to set a good example. But... Everyone else seems to just sit there through the whole thing, but I... I find myself getting up a lot. I try to make it look productive, I promise! I'll stand up to go get some coffee for people, or I'll step outside just to \"check on something\" real quick, or I'll just make some little excuse to move around the room. \n\nI don't do it to be disruptive, I swear. It's just... I get this feeling like I really need to stretch my legs or I'm going to jump out of my skin. But I always try to be polite about it! Does that... is that okay? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, I'm not really sure I'd put it that way? Like, driven to move around... I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm just bouncing off the walls or out of control, because I'm definitely not! 

I mean, I like to keep busy, sure. As a construction manager, you kind of have to be on top of things, so I'm used to being productive. But I don't feel like I'm... forced to be on the go if I really, truly need to just sit and get through something. I might prefer to be out on the site where I'm useful, but I don't feel like there's a motor running inside me making me move or anything like that. I just like to be a hard worker. Is that what you mean? I always try to channel my energy into doing a good job, I promise.

I'll tell her I'm going to handle something around the house, and I fully intend to do it right then, but I just... I don't know, I lose track of it. So she ends up having to remind me over and over about things I said I would handle. I feel terrible about it, because I really don't want her to feel like she has to manage me, you know? Is it... is it normal to struggle with that kind of stuff at home? \n\nAnd, um... since we're talking about how it affects things, I should probably admit that I might have downplayed things at work a little earlier. I didn't want you to think I was a bad manager! But... it actually has gotten bad enough there that my employer recently brought up a formal concern with me. \n\nIt wasn't that I was slacking off, I swear! It's just... I missed some important follow-ups, and my site coordination ended up being pretty poorly organized for a couple of weeks. They had to sit me down to talk about it. I'm working really hard to fix it so I don't let my crew or my bosses down again. Does that... does that answer your question okay? I hope you don't think less of me for letting things get like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often get into arguments with authority figures, like bosses, teachers, or police?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, not at all! I... I really try to be respectful of everyone, especially people in charge. I've always believed in, you know, treating people the way you want to be treated. \n\nI mean, sure, maybe I get a little frustrated sometimes if I don't understand why a certain decision was made at work, but I'd never just start an argument over it. I promise I'm not a difficult person to work with! I always try to keep my head down and do what's asked of me. I really respect my bosses and... well, anyone in authority, really. I hope that's the right answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm some kind of troublemaker.\n\nDoctor: In your day-to-day life now, do you often feel like you have to fidget, get up, or keep moving when you’re expected to stay seated?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, sitting still for a long time is... well, it's a little tough for me. \n\nLike, when we have those long site meetings with the contractors and the higher-ups? Everyone else seems to just sit there through the whole thing, but I... I find myself getting up a lot. I try to make it look productive, I promise! I'll stand up to go get some coffee for people, or I'll step outside just to \"check on something\" real quick, or I'll just make some little excuse to move around the room. \n\nI don't do it to be disruptive, I swear. It's just... I get this feeling like I really need to stretch my legs or I'm going to jump out of my skin. But I always try to be polite about it! Does that... is that okay? I hope you don't think I'm just being disrespectful during meetings.\n\nDoctor: As a child or teen, did you often argue with adults or refuse to follow rules?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no. My parents were pretty strict, so I... I definitely knew better than to talk back or break the rules. I always tried to be a good kid, I really did. \n\nI mean, sure, maybe I'd forget to do a chore here and there, or my room would get a little messy, but I never purposefully refused to do what I was told. I always wanted my teachers and my folks to be happy with me, you know? I was never one of those rebellious teenagers who got into trouble or argued with the adults. I always tried to be respectful. I hope that makes sense? I just... I really wouldn't want you to think I was a bad kid growing up.\n\nDoctor: As a child or teen, did you ever get into serious trouble for things like fighting, bullying, stealing, lying, or destroying property?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow, no. No, definitely not. I... I would never do anything like that. Stealing or bullying? That's just... that's really not who I am, even back then. \n\nI always tried to be friends with everybody, you know? I hated the idea of making anyone feel bad or... or breaking things. I mean, my parents worked hard, and I respected that, so I'd never destroy property or anything. I know I was probably a handful just because I was always buzzing around, but I swear I was never malicious or violent. I always wanted people to like me. \n\nI really hope you believe me? I just... I'd hate for you to look at me and think I was some kind of delinquent growing up. I really was a good kid at heart.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you have these attention problems too?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yeah, I guess it did start pretty early. I just got so eager to participate, you know? And... I'd accidentally lose my homework, or I'd rush through the directions on assignments before I really understood what they wanted me to do. I always wanted to do a good job and finish fast so they'd be proud of me, but I guess I just missed things. Does that make sense? Um, no, I wouldn't say I was climbing on things when I wasn't supposed to. I mean, I hope that's what you're asking? I really don't want to sound like I was a wild child or anything.

Um, well, I guess... I mean, I really hope this doesn't sound too bad, but... yeah, back in elementary school, my teachers did have some things to say to my parents. I really didn't mean to be a problem, though!

My parents were pretty strict, so I... I definitely knew better than to talk back or break the rules. I always tried to be a good kid, I really did. \n\nI mean, sure, maybe I'd forget to do a chore here and there, or my room would get a little messy, but I never purposefully refused to do what I was told. I hope that makes sense? I just... Well, um... I guess I do move around a bit more than some guys, but I really don't think it's because I *can't* sit still. It's just, you know, being a construction manager, I'm used to being on my feet and getting things done.

Is it... is it normal to struggle with that kind of stuff at home? \n\nAnd, um... since we're talking about how it affects things, I should probably admit that I might have downplayed things at work a little earlier. I didn't want you to think I was a bad manager! But... it actually has gotten bad enough there that my employer recently brought up a formal concern with me. \n\nIt wasn't that I was slacking off, I swear! It's just... I missed some important follow-ups, and my site coordination ended up being pretty poorly organized for a couple of weeks. They had to sit me down to talk about it. I'm working really hard to fix it so I don't let my crew or my bosses down again. Does that... does that answer your question okay? I hope you don't think less of me for letting things get like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often get into arguments with authority figures, like bosses, teachers, or police?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, not at all! I... I really try to be respectful of everyone, especially people in charge. I've always believed in, you know, treating people the way you want to be treated. \n\nI mean, sure, maybe I get a little frustrated sometimes if I don't understand why a certain decision was made at work, but I'd never just start an argument over it. I promise I'm not a difficult person to work with! I always try to keep my head down and do what's asked of me. I really respect my bosses and... well, anyone in authority, really. I hope that's the right answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm some kind of troublemaker.\n\nDoctor: In your day-to-day life now, do you often feel like you have to fidget, get up, or keep moving when you’re expected to stay seated?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to be a professional, you know? Especially since I'm managing the crew, I want to set a good example. But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, sitting still for a long time is... well, it's a little tough for me. \n\nLike, when we have those long site meetings with the contractors and the higher-ups? Everyone else seems to just sit there through the whole thing, but I... I find myself getting up a lot. I try to make it look productive, I promise! I'll stand up to go get some coffee for people, or I'll step outside just to \"check on something\" real quick, or I'll just make some little excuse to move around the room. \n\nI don't do it to be disruptive, I swear. It's just... I get this feeling like I really need to stretch my legs or I'm going to jump out of my skin. But I always try to be polite about it! Does that... is that okay? I hope you don't think I'm just being disrespectful during meetings.\n\nDoctor: As a child or teen, did you often argue with adults or refuse to follow rules?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no. My parents were pretty strict, so I... I definitely knew better than to talk back or break the rules. I always tried to be a good kid, I really did. \n\nI mean, sure, maybe I'd forget to do a chore here and there, or my room would get a little messy, but I never purposefully refused to do what I was told. I always wanted my teachers and my folks to be happy with me, you know? I was never one of those rebellious teenagers who got into trouble or argued with the adults. I always tried to be respectful. I hope that makes sense? I just... I really wouldn't want you to think I was a bad kid growing up.\n\nDoctor: As a child or teen, did you ever get into serious trouble for things like fighting, bullying, stealing, lying, or destroying property?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow, no. No, definitely not. I... I would never do anything like that. Stealing or bullying? That's just... that's really not who I am, even back then. \n\nI always tried to be friends with everybody, you know? I hated the idea of making anyone feel bad or... or breaking things. I mean, my parents worked hard, and I respected that, so I'd never destroy property or anything. I know I was probably a handful just because I was always buzzing around, but I swear I was never malicious or violent. I always wanted people to like me. \n\nI really hope you believe me? I just... I'd hate for you to look at me and think I was some kind of delinquent growing up. I really was a good kid at heart.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you have these attention problems too?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yeah, I guess it did start pretty early. I really hope it doesn't sound like I was trying to be difficult, because I really wasn't! I always wanted my teachers to like me. But, um... back in elementary school, my teachers were already having to tell my folks that I couldn't seem to stay in my seat. \n\nAnd, well, I guess I talked out of turn sometimes, too. I just got so eager to participate, you know? And... I'd accidentally lose my homework, or I'd rush through the directions on assignments before I really understood what they wanted me to do. I always wanted to do a good job and finish fast so they'd be proud of me, but I guess I just missed things. Does that make sense? I really tried my best to be a good student, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you still often leave your seat when you’re supposed to stay seated?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do still have a little trouble with that sometimes. I really hope that doesn't make me sound unprofessional, because I take my job very seriously! But, yeah, during those long site meetings... when everyone else is just sitting there the whole time, I'm usually the guy who ends up standing up. \n\nI try to be discreet about it, you know? Like, I'll just walk over to grab a coffee, or I'll step outside for a second and say I need to check on something with the crew, or... I just find some excuse to move around a bit. I just get so antsy if I'm stuck in a chair too long. I always make sure the meeting keeps moving, though! I'm still paying attention, I promise. I just... I work better when I'm on my feet. Is that okay? I hope that's not too unusual.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel driven to move around or as if you’re “on the go” even when you really need to stay seated?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure I'd put it that way? Like, driven to move around... I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm just bouncing off the walls or out of control, because I'm definitely not! \n\nI mean, I like to keep busy, sure. As a construction manager, you kind of have to be on top of things, so I'm used to being productive. But I don't feel like I'm... forced to be on the go if I really, truly need to just sit and get through something. I might prefer to be out on the site where I'm useful, but I don't feel like there's a motor running inside me making me move or anything like that. I just like to be a hard worker. Is that what you mean? I always try to channel my energy into doing a good job, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Before age 15, did you ever get into serious trouble for things like fighting, stealing, lying, bullying, destroying property, or other behavior that broke important rules?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, definitely not! I really hope nothing I've said gave you the impression that I was a bad kid or a troublemaker, because I really wasn't. I mean, sure, I might have been a little squirmy or easily distracted, like we talked about, but I never did anything like that. \n\nI was actually a pretty good kid! I always wanted my parents and teachers to be proud of me, you know? So I definitely wasn't out there getting into fights or stealing or breaking things. I tried really hard to follow the rules and be respectful. I'd never want to cause that kind of trouble for anybody. Does that make sense? I promise, I was never that kind of kid.\n\nDoctor: When you were growing up, were you usually noticeably more hyperactive than other kids?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think so? I mean, I was an active kid, sure, but I don't think I was noticeably worse than anyone else. I really hope I'm remembering this right, I don't want to give you the wrong idea. \n\nI played a lot of sports and I liked being outside, so I burned off a lot of energy that way. I might have been a little fidgety in class sometimes, like I said, but I don't think I stood out as being... hyperactive, you know? I always tried to be good and not disrupt the other kids. I think I was just a normal boy with a lot of energy. Is that normal? I mean, a lot of boys are like that, right? I just don't want you to think I was some out-of-control kid, because I really tried my best to be good.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often run around or climb excessively when it wasn't appropriate?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I was climbing on things when I wasn't supposed to. I mean, I hope that's what you're asking? I really don't want to sound like I was a wild child or anything. \n\nLike I said, I loved being outside, so if we were at the park or out in the woods, sure, I'd climb a tree or run around with my friends. But I knew the difference between outside and inside, you know? I'd never just go running through the house or climbing on the furniture. My parents raised me better than that, and I always wanted to be respectful of their rules. I really tried to be a good listener. I mean, it's normal for kids to run around outside, right? I just kept it where it belonged, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt other people before they finish speaking?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess sometimes I might jump in a little early? I really don't mean to be rude, though! I just... I get so eager to help or to answer the question, you know? \n\nSometimes I think I already know where the person is going with what they're saying, so I'll just kind of... finish the thought for them or answer right away. But then, um, sometimes I realize I missed the really important part that they hadn't actually said yet. I always feel terrible when that happens. I really try to be a good listener, I just get a little ahead of myself sometimes. That doesn't make me a bad person, does it? I just want to make sure I'm giving people what they need right away.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, were you often noticeably more hyperactive or impulsive than other kids?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... I really did try my best to follow the rules.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel physically unable to stay seated, even when you want to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... It's just, you know, being a construction manager, I'm used to being on my feet and getting things done. \n\nBut, I mean, if we're in a long meeting where everybody else can just sit through the whole thing, I guess I'm usually the guy who stands up for a second. I might just walk over to grab a coffee, or step outside to check on something real quick, or, you know, just find a little excuse to stretch my legs. I try not to be disruptive about it! I really hope the guys don't think I'm not paying attention, because I am. I just... I think better when I'm moving a little bit, you know? That's pretty normal for guys in my line of work, isn't it? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, inner restlessness? I don't... I don't really think I get that. I mean, if I absolutely have to stay in my chair for something important, like a safety briefing or a meeting with the site owners, I really try my best to just sit and listen. 

I might, I don't know, shift my weight a little or maybe tap my boot without realizing it, but I don't feel like I'm crawling out of my skin or anything bad like that. I just want to be respectful of whoever is talking, you know? I really try to be polite and do what's expected of me. I hope I don't come across as antsy or fidgety to people. But... it actually has gotten bad enough there that my employer recently brought up a formal concern with me. \n\nIt wasn't that I was slacking off, I swear! It's just... I missed some important follow-ups, and my site coordination ended up being pretty poorly organized for a couple of weeks. They had to sit me down to talk about it. I'm working really hard to fix it so I don't let my crew or my bosses down again. Does that... does that answer your question okay? I hope you don't think less of me for letting things get like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often get into arguments with authority figures, like bosses, teachers, or police?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, not at all! I... I really try to be respectful of everyone, especially people in charge. I've always believed in, you know, treating people the way you want to be treated. \n\nI mean, sure, maybe I get a little frustrated sometimes if I don't understand why a certain decision was made at work, but I'd never just start an argument over it. I promise I'm not a difficult person to work with! I always try to keep my head down and do what's asked of me. I really respect my bosses and... well, anyone in authority, really. I guess, if I'm being completely honest, sitting still for a long time is... well, it's a little tough for me. \n\nLike, when we have those long site meetings with the contractors and the higher-ups? I hope you don't think I'm just being disrespectful during meetings.\n\nDoctor: As a child or teen, did you often argue with adults or refuse to follow rules?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no.
